# KingbaseConnector Development Progress

Last updated: 2026-11-26. This is the single current implementation and acceptance tracker. Requirements are defined in the [feature matrix](kbconnector-feature-matrix.md); implementation workflow and completion rules are in the [development guide](kbconnector-development.md).

## 1. Current delivery and acceptance

The current implementation increment is E70: catalog enum and SET text values (see its evidence entry below). E69 added catalog and identifier scalar types, and E68 delivered XML binary COPY with Unicode declaration normalization. E54-E56 add native scalar/temporal paths, E57 notifications, E58 structured notices, E59 text/raw binary COPY, E60 transactional OID streams, E61 typed ordinary LOB values, E62 public PG-wire SequentialAccess with binary/text field readers and lazy batches, and E63 buffered text COPY lifetime checks. Remaining typed COPY codecs, strong streaming KeyInfo, types and metadata retain their acceptance limits. Complete mode/type/backend parity remains open. Read the delivery and mode tables first. The 122 parent requirements are broad contracts; their closure counts are not a development percentage or a test count.

### Delivered implementation by functional area

Recent accepted deliveries on master are E59 text/raw COPY (`5448ed5`), E60 transactional OID streams (`dcc7173`), E61 ordinary LOB binding (`c309238`), E62 network sequential Reader (`1355734`) and E63 COPY lifetime correction (`0c07d76`). E64 adds streaming parameter input, E65 adds typed binary COPY core, E66 adds exact numeric/PG temporal codecs, E67 adds JSON/JSONB and PG bit/varbit codecs, E68 adds XML, E69 adds catalog/identifier scalar types, and E70 adds catalog enum/SET text values; verification is recorded below. These increments deliver working APIs; none closes every mode, backend, platform and failure case of its parent requirement. Complete parent acceptance remains a separate gate.

The old report of "1 complete out of 122" mixed two different questions: what code has been delivered, and which complete requirements have passed every declared acceptance gate. It understated the delivered work as a progress report. The functional-area table answers the first question; the closure inventory answers the second. The 122 requirements include substantial missing product functionality as well as acceptance gaps; they are not predominantly test tasks, and the remaining work cannot be explained by stale documentation alone.

E62 implements public SequentialAccess Reader, forward scalar and bounded binary/text field readers, command/Reader lifetime transfer and lazy sequential batches, together with the internal DataRow wire/state machine, shared preparation plans and incremental bytea decoding. Its original verification passes 2,316 protocol +497 unit cases per modern runtime (8,439 executions), five exact package consumers, full .NET analyzers and independent review. Its source-built LIVE passes 341 cases per runtime, 1,023 executions with zero failures/skips. The public streaming cases use all four modes and include ordinary Oracle/MySQL LOB columns; exact package probes remain simulated. E64 adds ordinary streaming input. Strong streaming KeyInfo and broader backend/platform/profile acceptance remain open; current regression counts appear below.

E60 delivers the transactional OID large-object manager and streams (B04): create/open/read/write/64-bit seek/length/truncate/close/unlink, savepoint-aware descriptor ownership and pooled borrower isolation. E61 delivers ordinary BLOB/CLOB/NCLOB byte-array/string input, prepared/qualified-name/NULL paths and buffered partial reads on proven Oracle/MySQL domains. E62 adds network output streaming and E64 adds caller-owned Stream/TextReader input with temporary staging and a 64MiB Bind-frame limit. B04 is Implemented with broader acceptance pending; B05 remains In progress because complete LOB bounds, native-backend and profile/platform acceptance remain open.

E59-E68 have full regression, five exact-package consumer, .NET analyzer, independent review and source-built LIVE evidence recorded per increment below. Text/raw COPY, OID streams and E64 ordinary streaming input handle data beyond the buffered query limit; ordinary byte-array/string input retains that limit. The supplied server uses KBCOPY headers; MySQL's observed invalid-integer-to-zero conversion differs from other modes' 22P02. Remaining typed binary row codecs, full error-tail/profile/platform acceptance and successful MySQL zero-calendar acceptance remain open.

"Implemented subset" means the named public/code path exists and has the stated evidence. It does not close the larger feature ID or accept every mode, type, platform or failure scenario.

### Verification baseline

E70's regression record is 2,685 protocol +497 unit cases on each of net8/net9/net10: 9,546 runtime executions, not distinct features. Full source-built LIVE is 489 cases per host, 1,467 executions, with zero failures/skips. E69 recorded 2,664 protocol and 481 LIVE cases; E68 recorded 2,634 and 461. Offline runs separately skip 169 LIVE methods per host by design. Five exact package assets run on Windows; Standard assets execute on .NET 8. These records do not establish Linux, Framework, ARM64, exact-package LIVE or complete four-mode support. See E69 for current commands and evidence limits; earlier entries record their original baselines.

E51 closes scoped PG adapter batching; E52-E58 add four-mode execution/pooling, native scalars and events; E59 adds text/raw COPY, E60 OID streams, E61 bounded ordinary LOB values, E62 network sequential output, E64 streaming input, E65 typed binary COPY core, E66 exact numeric/PG temporal COPY, E67 JSON/PG bit COPY, E68 XML COPY, E69 catalog/identifier scalar types and E70 catalog enum/SET text values. Remaining work includes additional COPY codecs, strong streaming KeyInfo, enum/SET/array types, mode-specific types/metadata/package state, routines/output parameters/cursors, native MySQL and broader acceptance. Q15 joins B01/B03/B04/B06 as Implemented for the named PG-wire paths; B05/Q16 and Q17 retain their complete LOB/metadata and phase/outcome/error-tail contracts.

Scope inventory: 85 V9 required (R), 26 V9 conditional (C), 7 later-scope (L), and 4 subsequent V8 features. G01 is the only fully closed parent. C01/G02/G03/B01/B03/B04/B06/Q15 are classified Implemented from their configuration/factory/package/API/COPY/OID/notification/streaming Reader code and recorded evidence; missing applicability-specific acceptance remains visible. Other delivered mode-specific subsets remain inside open parent contracts rather than disappearing into a single undifferentiated count. No overall completion percentage is inferred from these unequal-sized rows. Conditional applicability must be resolved individually; missing environments do not mean Not applicable.

Reporting rule: start progress reports with concrete delivered APIs, commits and their remaining gaps, then report whole-requirement closure separately. The 122 rows are functional contracts, not a test backlog. The old E51-era count (1 complete, 64 in progress, 46 not started, 11 deferred) is historical and superseded by this table. A low complete count can reflect unfinished acceptance as well as unfinished implementation; neither reason may hide the other. Tests executed are evidence counts, not delivered-feature counts.

### Next implementation and acceptance work

1. Extend B02 beyond the E65-E68 base-codec subset: other base/native/domain/complex formats and streaming fields require their own verified contracts. Strong streaming KeyInfo remains explicit open Q16 scope. E69 and E70 closed the catalog blockers that made `SELECT current_user`, `pg_catalog` and MySQL-mode `information_schema` reads fail; the two `sys.binary` columns of MySQL-mode `information_schema.routines` still need a native binary contract. Choose the next bounded increment from these or the remaining V9 contracts, with tests, four-mode evidence where applicable, documentation and independent review before local commit.
2. Continue missing V9 product contracts, including remaining mode-specific types and catalogs, routines/output parameters/cursors, configuration/authentication/transports, transaction features and the explicit native MySQL backend. Their feature rows remain the authority; this list does not reduce scope or imply all dependencies can run in parallel.
3. Close the corresponding profile, package-consumer, platform, resource/fault and release-readiness acceptance gaps. Record actual capability or environment limitations individually. Keep unsupported real-server TLS acceptance deferred and offline TLS tests active.
4. Begin the documented V8 adaptation only after the V9 gate. Publishing, pushing and tagging remain outside the current authorization.

Current handoff: development resumed with the user's own four-mode V9 installation available as the LIVE endpoint, and local commits remain authorized after tests, documentation synchronization and code review. E63 corrects buffered text COPY lifetime handling and stale handoff statements. E64 delivers ordinary Stream/TextReader parameter input; E65 delivers typed binary COPY core, E66 adds exact numeric/PG temporal codecs, E67 adds JSON/PG bit codecs, E68 adds XML codecs, E69 adds catalog/identifier scalar types and E70 adds catalog enum/SET text values, with further codecs and types explicitly open. T23 stays In progress for ordered per-type label validation, domain codecs and native binary types. Real-server TLS acceptance remains user-deferred because the supplied endpoints lack TLS; offline TLS tests remain required. Private configuration, credentials, deployment identities and raw LIVE records stay outside the repository and outside tracked files. No push, tag or release publication is authorized.

Documentation deliverables are tracked separately from product functionality:

- [x] Maintain the unified design and feature matrix in English, with official sources and explicit evidence limits.
- [x] Provide an implementation guide with dependencies, validation workflow, and completion rules.
- [x] Register all 122 feature IDs in the progress ledger with an initial truthful status.
- [x] Extend the default-PG design for exceptional explicit MySQL-only connections and built-in pooling; synchronize requirements and work packages. This is documentation completion only.
- [x] Define startup decision gates and the first usable PG increment in development guide section 3.1. Toolchain/API decisions and live discovery remain work-package tasks; no product acceptance is implied.

## 3. Work packages

Work-package checkboxes can be marked complete when the named subset and its exit condition are satisfied. A package that implements only part of a feature must identify that subset in its evidence entry; the full feature remains open in section 4 until all requirements pass. Dependencies below define development order, not additional scope or already implemented behavior.

- [x] **DP01 - Engineering and package structure** (V9-1; G01, G02). SDK/solution/shared props/CPM/core and test projects build all five TFMs; offline checks, packaging and explicit asset consumers execute. E18 delivers the separate DI package and usable unpooled DataSource lifetime; G02/G03/G08 remain partial. Evidence: E01/E18/E22; E22 adds the CI/release automation, with hosted execution pending.
- [ ] **DP02 - Server profiles and bootstrap discovery** (V9-0; C03-C05, G08). In progress: documented version query/grammar and offline version/mode probe E08, with trusted sys_catalog function resolution in E23. Register authorized profiles and confirm raw/product output, mode, catalog, editions and routine facts; establish isolated official comparison entry points. E29 establishes bounded four-mode bootstrap and PG public evidence; full profile/build/deployment acceptance remains open.
- [ ] **DP03 - Connection, configuration, and authentication foundation** (V9-1; C01-C02, C06-C10, C16, C18-C20, G06, G11). In progress: configuration (E02), offline password/MD5 (E04), SCRAM-SHA-256/SASLprep (E25), startup (E05), loopback TCP/platform TLS (E06) and internal physical initialization composition/shared deadline/startup settings (E10). Public unpooled PG connection/factory integration is exercised in E17; E24 adds one pooled Open budget spanning waiting/initialization/validation. E26 adds a DataSource password provider and bounded retrieval/rotation. E27 adds bounded configured CA-file trust with hostname/validity/EKU checks. Revocation/client certificates, passfile/environment sources, complete session configuration, remaining algorithms and live login remain open. Use DP02 evidence for identity-dependent behavior.
- [ ] **DP04 - Protocol state and failure handling** (V9-1; Q01, Q05-Q06, Q17-Q18, P06, G07). In progress: bounded framing/frontend/control-error parsers (E03), owned internal Simple Query (E07), catalog-bound text Extended Query (E13), public execution/Reader ownership and safe server exceptions (E17), and structured command/Wait Notice delivery/error precedence (E58). Complete extended capabilities, SQL Server Error/CommandComplete/Ready tails, cancellation-channel closure and uncertain-outcome contracts remain pending.
- [ ] **DP05 - Type system and basic codecs** (V9-1; T01-T03, T05, T07-T10, T15, T28, T30). In progress: required-built-in catalog/OID lookup E09, bounded scalar text conversion E11, exact Decimal/KingbaseNumeric text helper E12, actual-catalog input resolution E15 and buffered scalar/provider Reader access E16. Basic helpers use actual OIDs with stable CLR types and explicit NULL/unsupported behavior. E17 exposes this bounded scalar subset through public commands. E32 adds PG DATE text binding/Reader conversion, ISO DateStyle snapshots, exact CLR and lossless provider calendar values. E33 adds TIME/TIMETZ microsecond, end-of-day and explicit second-offset values with modern TimeOnly access. E34 adds TIMESTAMP/TIMESTAMPTZ Kind-aware binding, lossless provider values and exact UTC conversion with ISO snapshots. E35 adds the bounded PG INTERVAL text/provider/TimeSpan slice with independently confirmed IntervalStyle snapshots. E37 adds optional XML catalog/text binding and String/buffered TextReader results; E68 separately adds XML binary COPY. Remaining catalog/runtime formats, interval YM/DS and full server bounds, binary codecs and session facts remain open. Later mode-specific coverage for these features remains part of DP08.

E17 adds public unpooled Connection/Command ownership, scalar/nonquery/Reader and Default/SingleRow/SingleResult/CloseConnection behaviors. E52 adds bounded four-mode text planning/binding and public unpooled execution. E62 adds network SequentialAccess and lazy batches; strong streaming KeyInfo, remaining CommandBehavior and full mode metadata remain pending.

After DP20 and shared command/type contracts, implement prepared binding with session-aware lexical plans, text/binary/streaming results and applicable ENUM/SET, transaction/status/error semantics, native catalogs/schema/routines, and capability rejections for PG-only APIs. Verify supported subsets rather than assuming PG feature parity. Evidence: none.
- [ ] **DP22 - Both-protocol pool and exceptional endpoint acceptance** (V9-3; P01-P03, P06, C16, Q05, Q18, X04 where applicable, G06-G08; A16-A17). After DP07/DP20/DP21, validate per-backend reset or retirement, bounded cancellation, two-borrower state isolation, stress/clear/disposal/credential and registry-reclamation races, same-transaction reacquisition/completion on X04 profiles, and a MySQL-only endpoint with PG unavailable. Resolve U07 facts; retain profile-specific applicability and physical-reuse evidence. Evidence: none.

Work-package IDs are stable, not execution order. There are 22 packages; DP20-DP22 are dependencies of the V9 release package DP16. Shared features retain one canonical ledger row even when multiple packages implement different subsets.

## 4. Feature ledger

Each matrix feature has exactly one row below. The matrix owns names, scope, and acceptance requirements; keep IDs synchronized when it changes. `[ ]` is open and `[x]` means **Complete** under development guide section 6. Use Not started, In progress, Implemented, Blocked, Complete, Deferred, or Not applicable for Status. Record an accepted profile/subset and evidence before checking a row.

SIM and LIVE are independent: Not run, Passed, Failed, or N/A with an explicit reason. A LIVE pass must name profiles in the evidence entry. N/A is not available merely because a server or test is missing. Deferred rows remain unchecked.

## 6. Validation and completion evidence

This section records implementation and acceptance evidence linked from the ledger/package rows. Keep test artifacts and temporary investigation output outside this document.

Use the following fields for each entry:

| Field | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Feature / work package | Canonical feature IDs, package ID, and exact sub-items covered. |
| State and date | Current status, completion date when applicable, and the declared acceptance boundary. |
| Source / artifact | Commit or stable source/artifact identity; implementation and test paths. Use package version/hash when validating a package. |
| Commands and results | Exact relevant commands, pass/fail/skip outcomes, and test/subcase IDs. |
| SIM | Executed simulation cases and limitations, or an evidence-based N/A reason. |
| LIVE | profileId and per-subcase results, or the unresolved requirement/inapplicability reason. |
| Comparison | Official DLL full hash and independent specification/observations if a comparison informed the result. |
| Failure/limits | Client defect, confirmed server limit, permissions/configuration, missing environment, or unknown; link defects and explain the evidence. |
| Resource/cleanup | Connection/transaction/Reader/COPY ownership and test data cleanup results where relevant. |
| Follow-up | Remaining requirements and blockers; leave the parent unchecked if any applicable requirement remains. |

When a unit is finished, update its row, package, phase, and section 1 counts in the same change. Preserve earlier evidence when reopening a regression or expanding the acceptance set. A passing local test, a matching official result, or a completed documentation task alone must not create a LIVE pass.

Async I/O requires a cancellation-aware stream; transport deadlines/abort, TLS/authentication, query dispatch/synchronization, the bounded PG cancellation-channel closure barrier, public exception execution phases, notices and large-row/COPY streaming remain open.

### E05 - DP03/DP04 internal bounded PG startup exchange (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: C06/C08/C18 and DP03/DP04 remain In progress. The internal coordinator sends StartupMessage, processes supported authentication, parameters/notices/key/error messages and returns only after an idle ReadyForQuery plus confirmed UTF8 and exactly one backend key. It does not select a transport, secure a socket, establish Kingbase identity or implement public Open.

- Contract/provenance: independently implements the W21/W22 PG startup phase using existing framing/authentication primitives and no additional dependencies. Bounds the exchange to 256 backend frames and 1 MiB aggregate payload. Latest repeated ParameterStatus values replace previous ones. Structured notices are parsed/discarded in this internal subset; public notice events remain open.
- Commands/results: 11 new tests failed against temporary unimplemented signatures before implementation. After the review correction below, `./eng/verify.ps1` passed: 78 protocol plus 55 unit cases on each net8.0/net9.0/net10.0 (399 passes), 3 visible live-gate skips, five product TFMs without warnings/errors and five explicit configuration/version package consumers. Internal startup runtime SIM uses modern assets; Standard assets compile only for this subset.
- SIM: one-byte-fragmented trust/password/MD5 startup, literal outgoing bytes, repeated parameters, notices at either phase, server errors before/after authentication, phase/order/key/encoding/transaction errors, unsupported algorithms with no password response, message/payload limits, pre-cancellation versus pending-read cancellation, password-buffer clearing on success/write failure and one-time stream retirement.
- LIVE/resources/follow-up: no real profile, socket or accepted LIVE result. The caller owns the successful wire; all failed exchanges after I/O retire it, preserving the original error. Returned password frames are cleared after the write even if it throws. Remaining transport/TLS, complete algorithms, session settings, public connection/exception/notice contracts, identity/catalog initialization and pool/reset/DI lifecycle remain pending.
- Code review: independent review identified that the first payload-budget check happened after frame materialization. A header-only oversized-frame test reproduced the stall. Framing now checks each read against the remaining startup payload allowance before allocation/body reads; exact/zero/negative limits and sync/async body-unread cases are covered. Independent re-review found no further actionable issues; full verification passed after this fix.

### E06 - DP03 TCP and platform TLS transport (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: C02/C07 and G07 transport/resource subsets In progress; DP03 remains open. Internal synchronous/asynchronous TCP connection and strict PG SSLRequest upgrade are implemented. Public Open, a shared initialization deadline, startup integration and Kingbase identity are pending.
- Source/artifact: `Protocol/PgTransport.cs`, `PgTransportTests.cs` and design section 6.5. Local `KingbaseConnector.0.1.0.nupkg` SHA-256 `557020A3204520C0999FE3D59A23D48230EDBD8C55E47A04BF87019BFF0C90D4`.
- Contract/provenance: independently implements W21/W23 with BCL TcpClient/SslStream and no new dependencies. Disable sends no SSLRequest; Require encrypts without certificate validation; VerifyFull uses system chain/hostname validation. Both TLS modes require an S reply and protected TLS, with no plaintext fallback. Every asset explicitly selects OS TLS protocols with SslProtocols.None. Revocation checking is disabled in this subset.
- Commands/results: 14 new cases failed against temporary unimplemented signatures before implementation. `./eng/verify.ps1` passed after implementation and fixture correction: 93 protocol plus 55 unit cases on each net8.0/net9.0/net10.0 (444 passes), 3 visible live-gate skips, five product TFMs without warnings/errors and five explicit configuration/version package consumers. Windows x64 SDK 10.0.403; runtimes 8.0.33/9.0.22/10.0.14. Internal Standard transport is compiled, not directly exercised.
- SIM: 15 loopback transport cases per modern runtime cover sync/async plaintext and encrypted round trips, exact SSLRequest bytes, untrusted-certificate rejection before application data, N/invalid/truncated replies without fallback, cancellation during SSL negotiation/TLS handshake, pre-cancel and input validation, token lifetime after success, stream ownership and repeated disposal. IPv6 literal ::1 ran successfully on this host; unsupported IPv6 hosts visibly skip that case.
- Failure diagnosis: the first TLS fixture used an ephemeral server key rejected by Windows Schannel. The server error confirmed this fixture limitation; exporting/reloading its test-only PFX with UserKeySet corrected it without changing client validation or machine trust. Exported private-key bytes are cleared, certificates disposed, and peer failures awaited rather than hidden by client EOF.
- LIVE/resources/follow-up: no registered Kingbase profile or LIVE result. The transport owns its socket/stream; failures and in-flight cancellation close both, while later cancellation of the creation token leaves a successful transport open. Custom CA/client certificates, isolated hostname-mismatch and positive trusted-chain tests, revocation policy, Standard/older-host DNS cancellation latency, shared deadlines, identity, pooling and DI remain pending. No database objects or trust-store changes were made.
- Code review: independent read-only review found no actionable defects; its suggestion to use the explicit Standard TLS overload was applied and accepted in follow-up review, together with IPv6 coverage. Full verification passed after the final source/test changes.

### E07 - DP04 owned bounded PG text-query exchanges (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: Q01/Q05/Q06/Q17/Q18 and C06/G07 internal subsets In progress. PgQuerySession owns an authenticated UTF8 wire and executes one bounded synchronous/asynchronous Simple Query exchange at a time. Public Command/Reader, Extended Query, typed codecs, cancellation-channel synchronization and pooling remain open.
- Source/artifact: `Protocol/PgQuerySession.cs`, `PgQuerySessionTests.cs` and design section 7. Local `KingbaseConnector.0.1.0.nupkg` SHA-256 `3A70E1BC6F291D122602A1D005D760AF125CD9485EE63A43A04B736C2461F150`.
- Contract/provenance: independently implements W21/W22 text RowDescription/DataRow, CommandComplete, EmptyQueryResponse and ReadyForQuery using existing BCL-only framing. Retains signed metadata values, raw unsigned OIDs, strict UTF8, NULL versus empty text, separate statement results and command tags. No static OID/type-catalog assumptions or identity queries. The exchange returns results only at ReadyForQuery; ordinary server errors discard results, drain to ReadyForQuery and throw safe SQLSTATE text before another query is allowed.
- Bounds: 1 MiB outbound UTF8 SQL including terminator; 1 MiB aggregate backend payload enforced from frame headers before body allocation; 4096 frames, 256 results, 1024 rows and 1024 columns per result. Session parameter retention is independently capped at 256 entries and 1 MiB UTF8 name/value bytes including terminators across repeated queries; replacing values releases previous allowance.
- Commands/results: 54 initial cases failed against temporary unimplemented signatures. Four additional empty-query phase cases and two retained-parameter cases reproduced missing checks before correction. `./eng/verify.ps1` passed after the final additions: 156 protocol plus 55 unit cases per net8.0/net9.0/net10.0 (633 passes), 3 visible live-gate skips, all five product TFMs without warnings/errors and five explicit configuration/version package consumers. Windows x64 SDK 10.0.403; Standard internal query paths compile, while query runtime cases execute on modern assets.
- SIM: 63 new cases per modern host cover one-byte fragmentation, unknown raw OIDs/signed metadata, Unicode/NULL/empty rows, zero-column/zero-row/nonquery/empty/multiple results, immutable returned data, repeated session parameters and notices, exact/over budgets, every truncated metadata/row payload, malformed counts/lengths/UTF8/trailing bytes/phases, unsupported binary/COPY/async messages, pre-cancel/local SQL validation, concurrent-exchange rejection, failed writes, truncated I/O, cancellation and disposal while reading. A TCP loopback case composes transport, trust startup and text query on the same socket and verifies peer EOF after session disposal.
- Error recovery/review: independent connected-source review found no actionable issues. A possible EmptyQueryResponse/ErrorResponse ordering concern was checked against W22's rule that errors/notices may interrupt any response phase; two explicit tests now verify errors after completed/empty results are drained and reported before reuse. This does not allow additional statement results after EmptyQueryResponse. A review reflection probe failed setup and contributed no behavioral evidence; acceptance relies on the executed owning tests.
- LIVE/resources/follow-up: no real Kingbase profile or LIVE result. Initial local validation, pre-cancellation and rejected concurrent calls preserve the session; failures after exchange start retire it unless a valid ReadyForQuery confirmed error synchronization. Socket/wire disposal is idempotent and blocked test reads terminate. The review's remaining boundaries are U01/U02 identity/mode/catalog, typed/public execution, COPY/streaming/cancellation channels/pooling, functional Connection/DataSource/DI and broader authentication/TLS/deployment/framework-host acceptance. Those remain pending, not judged complete by this module.

Raw text remains available through the existing query result.

### E24 - DP07 ordinary public PG pooling and lease ownership (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: P01/P03 move to In progress; P02/P06/C02/C19 and DP07 retain partial status. Public Pooling=true is now the default ordinary PG path for direct connections and DataSources; Pooling=false retains dedicated physical creation/close. System transaction enlistment remains explicitly rejected. This implements public pool lifecycles against simulated peers, without accepting any actual Kingbase reset profile.
- Source/artifact: PgSessionPool, PgPoolRegistry, connection lease/async cleanup integration, DataSource private ownership, canonical connection-string keys, four lifecycle/capacity test files plus cancellation regressions, configuration tests and all five package consumers; README/design section 9. Local core 0.1.0 nupkg SHA-256 `ACF1CC92EE05C48594323D7A98913365E4D46CB366574D5DDEBFC345ED81D69C`; DI nupkg SHA-256 `EA95E0B1DF8E04C07F577A22459E55785957BB41B9DDE28151ED07E76F2C2F86`.
- Identity/capacity: private pools never merge with each other or direct construction. Direct keys normalize all defaults, aliases/order/enums and DNS host casing while retaining database/user/password case and all policy differences. FIFO waiters reserve capacity before initialization; opening/leased/resetting/retiring/old-generation entries count until reuse or completed physical disposal. Cancellation removes a queued waiter or retires its granted slot exactly once. One monotonic Open budget covers queue, physical initialization and SELECT 1 idle validation. Failed idle validation can reacquire a new session; application SQL is never replayed.
- Ownership/cleanup: Close/CloseAsync revokes Reader/transaction/command generations before E23 reset; active interrupted exchanges retire without concurrent reset. Repeated close joins one cleanup; Open rejects while that cleanup is active. Stale children and old opening failures cannot touch a later lease. ChangeDatabase returns the captured lease to its original pool before opening the new database. Modern assets override BCL async close/dispose; Standard2.0 exposes typed Task counterparts. Reset failure destroys the physical connection before freeing capacity.
- Clearing/lifetime: ClearPool(connection), ClearAllPools and DataSource.ClearPool increment generations and immediately close idle sessions. Active leases survive clear/source disposal and retire on return; source disposal rejects new opens and wakes waiters. Lazy minimum, idle/total age checks and weak timer pruning are implemented. Explicit password changes isolate direct authentication contexts; empty direct pools reclaim atomically only without entries/waiters/rents and clear stored configuration. Callback credentials and system-transaction reservations are not implemented.
- SIM/tests: initial seven public pool cases failed against the former Pooling=true rejection, then passed. Expanded coverage includes max-one FIFO/cancel/timeout, failed initialization/reset and retained capacity, clear during opening/reset, source disposal, duplicate close/open exclusion, broken idle replacement, aliases/defaults and private/password isolation, registry reclamation, idle/minimum/total expiry and background pruning, old Reader/transaction/cancel protection, active-exchange retirement, queued close/reopen and reentrant StateChange. ### E25 - DP03 PG SCRAM-SHA-256 login and Unicode 3.2 password preparation (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: C09 moves to In progress; DP03 remains open. The existing PG authentication owner now accepts a validated SCRAM-SHA-256 offer, Continue and verified Final before AuthenticationOk. Public dedicated and pooled physical creation use this path automatically when requested by the server. No new connection setting, product dependency, SDK pin or version change is introduced. SCRAM-SHA-256-PLUS/channel binding, SM3 algorithms and real Kingbase password-policy acceptance remain pending.
- Source/artifact: PgScramSha256, PgSaslPrep and its generated Unicode 3.2 tables; disposable PgPasswordAuthentication and token-aware PgStartup; development-only Generate-SaslPrepTables.py; SCRAM/state/lifetime/startup/password-profile tests, README, design 6.5/W32-W33 and matrix source index. Local core 0.1.0 nupkg SHA-256 `B37CD92EE572CE064676A9CF28C62D0DFD3E96A0D42FC3B81E8EAB409F86DEF0`; DI nupkg SHA-256 `37D2421DE3BFFAF3AB0D0586E481EA51C254496960939F797B5FE8FFAE074B0F`.
- Protocol/crypto: validate the complete mechanism list, select standard SHA-256 without subsequent algorithm switching/fallback, and reject PLUS-only offers. Generate a fresh cryptographic 24-byte nonce per new authentication; require a longer matching server nonce. Validate strict UTF8, unique ordered core attributes, positive decimal grammar, canonical Base64 and unknown mandatory extensions; retain optional extension bytes in the proof transcript. Derive SHA-256/HMAC proof with a one-block PBKDF2 loop and verify all 32 server-signature bytes without early mismatch exit. AuthenticationOk before the verified Final cannot complete startup. Bound SASL payloads to 16 KiB, including initial mechanism/length overhead, salt to 1 KiB and iterations to 1..1,000,000 before costly work. These are client resource limits, not measured server maxima; RFC 7677 recommends server iterations of at least 4096.
- Password/cancellation/ownership: PG Startup username remains authoritative; the client-first username escapes comma/equal. Apply RFC 4013 mappings, frozen Unicode 3.2 classifications, NFKC including five later-corrected decomposition forms, prohibition/unassigned and bidi checks. Preparation failure uses the original UTF8 password as described by the PG reference; existing NUL/invalid-UTF16 validation still rejects locally. The generator uses Python's Unicode 3.2 stringprep data, reproduces identical tables on rerun and adds no Python product/runtime requirement. PBKDF2 checks the existing initialization token every 64 iterations, so caller/Open-budget cancellation interrupts CPU work. Startup disposes the auth owner on every exit; outgoing SASL frames are cleared after successful/failed writes, expected signatures after verification/failure/disposal, and temporary password/derived-key/proof arrays in finally blocks. Immutable configured/prepared/transcript strings cannot be erased.
- Static/SIM evidence: RFC 5802/7677, RFC 4013/3454 and PG14 SASL references returned HTTP 200 and are cached under `artifacts/validation/scram-*.txt`. The literal RFC 7677 exchange matches initial response, client proof and server signature. Tests reject malformed mechanism lists, unsupported-only lists, nonce mismatch/no extension, ordering/duplicates/mandatory extensions, iteration zero/sign/leading-zero/overflow/cap violations, noncanonical/oversized salt, invalid UTF8, error/bad/short signatures, premature Ok and algorithm/phase switching. Unicode examples cover mapping/case/NFKC, frozen unassigned characters, prohibited/bidi raw fallback and all five normalization corrections. Independent BCL PBKDF2 peer computations validate empty/prepared passwords, short salt and iteration endpoints. Loopback public sync/async login proceeds through identity/catalog/query only after authentication; failed proof/EOF never publishes Open. Pooled reuse does not reauthenticate; clearing creates a fresh nonce/authentication. Token/deadline, nonces, write-failure clearing and retained signature-buffer erasure have explicit tests. This is SIM only, not Kingbase server acceptance.
- Tests/results: the first SCRAM suite ran against unsupported authentication and failed 53 cases while two existing unsupported-only outcomes passed. SASLprep vectors failed 12 cases before preparation was implemented. The initial-response aggregate-bound regression later failed before the mechanism/length overhead was included, then passed. Final targeted net8 suite passes 108 cases. Final `./eng/verify.ps1 -ContinuousIntegration` exited 0: 916 protocol +199 unit cases per net8/net9/net10 = 3,345 passes, three visible LIVE-gate skips, both products across all five TFMs with zero build warnings/errors, both packages, version/provenance/dependency-floor gates and five fresh-cache exact-archive consumers passed. Standard assets execute consumer checks on .NET 8; no full Framework-host protocol acceptance is inferred. `git diff --check` passed. ### E26 - DP03 DataSource password provider and physical-creation rotation (2026-11-24)

- Scope/state: C16 moves to In progress; G11/DP03 and full pooling acceptance remain open. KingbaseDataSourceBuilder.PasswordProvider captures a Task<string>-returning KingbasePasswordProvider at Build. Its immutable KingbasePasswordContext exposes Host/Port/Database/Username/WireProtocol only. Explicitly stored Password/Pwd conflicts, including typed/quoted empty values, reject before builder serialization. Existing BCL semantics treat unquoted Password= as removing that setting; Remove explicitly clears a prior setting. Direct construction remains explicit-password only; provider identity is scoped to each private DataSource/pool.
- Source/artifact: KingbasePasswordProvider/context/safe exception, PgPasswordProvider, DataSource/Connection/private pool and physical initializer integration, provider unit/protocol tests, five asset consumers, README and design 6.5. Local core 0.1.0 nupkg SHA-256 `8830579F6BBBE4CEA2F86DB2A00C9D4E0170CD509658FABDD507C6235939910C`; DI nupkg SHA-256 `DF4CDABE861EAD0BEFA8E85C39966AC678CA817DB6610D78F6CA93577C92D8C8`.
- Execution/ownership: Build, connection creation and DI resolution do not invoke the provider. Each attempted new physical initialization fetches once before TCP under the existing Open token/deadline, including earlier pool waiting, and validates text/aggregate credential limits before network I/O. Empty returned passwords are valid. Reuse, SELECT 1 idle validation and ordinary DISCARD reset skip retrieval; clearing/replacement therefore fetches current credentials without mutating existing leases. Returned passwords are not written to source/connection strings, registry keys or reusable session/reset state. No periodic credential cache/refresh option or new runtime dependency is introduced.
- Cancellation/errors: worker dispatch keeps provider code away from pool locks and synchronous caller synchronization contexts. Waiting is cancellable even if the provider's synchronous prefix blocks or returned task ignores its token. External provider work can outlive cancellation; abandoned faults are observed and late completion cannot initiate transport, publish a connection or affect a subsequent lease. Callbacks must support concurrent invocations and should honor cancellation. Caller cancellation, source disposal and Open timeout retain their existing classifications. Provider exceptions, unrelated cancellation, null task/result and invalid credentials become KingbasePasswordProviderException with fixed safe text and no inner exception or stale-password fallback. Immutable password strings cannot be erased.
- Tests/review: tests first failed against the missing provider execution/conflict behavior. The first configuration check also exposed BCL empty-value serialization; validation now checks the original builder's stored fields before snapshot serialization, preserving BCL removal semantics and rejecting typed/quoted empty conflicts. ### E27 - DP03 configured CA trust and verified TLS policy (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: resume development after the E26 shutdown checkpoint. C07/DP03, C02/G03/G07/G11 and platform acceptance remain In progress. Implement the existing Root Certificate option through direct/owned public PG connections, without a new API, dependency or SDK pin. Empty paths retain platform trust; nonempty paths require VerifyFull, while Require/Disable conflicts reject locally.
- Source/contract: PgCertificateTrust owns at most 32 CA certificates from a file of at most 1 MiB, loaded before TCP on each physical creation. Accept one DER certificate or a strict CERTIFICATE PEM bundle, require CA basic constraints and signing key usage when present, reject other content and require a self-issued root. Bundle intermediates supply chain-building material; configured self-issued roots alone are trust anchors. Reject malformed/unavailable files with fixed AuthenticationException text and no path, certificate contents or inner exception. No user/machine trust stores are changed.
- Chain/ownership: preserve SslStream hostname and missing-certificate checks. Modern assets apply CustomRootTrust, server-authentication purpose and disabled certificate downloads to the initial TLS policy and callback rebuild; Standard assets use ExtraStore/AllowUnknownCertificateAuthority plus a successful build and exact DER comparison of the final anchor. No other verification flags are relaxed. Transport owns the loaded certificate objects through session disposal; creation cancellation/failure disposes them and the socket. Shared Open cancellation/timeout still covers transport creation. Standard APIs cannot disable platform certificate downloads; certificate retrieval latency on untested legacy hosts is unaccepted. Revocation stays disabled as in E06; configurable revocation/client certificates are separate work.
- Pool/configuration: Build/DI remain lazy, and the built source retains its configured path snapshot. CA file changes do not alter established TLS sessions or ordinary pool reset/idle validation. Clear/replacement forces the next physical creation to reread the file. The pool identity includes Root Certificate. A simulated two-borrower private pool continued on one TLS socket after its file became invalid; clearing then rejected before TCP, and restoring the file allowed another physical session at maximum pool size one.
- Tests/evidence: initial new suite failed 21 cases against unsupported custom trust/missing configuration conflict validation, while two existing rejection paths passed. First implementation passed 60 custom-CA/transport/initializer cases on net8; expanded coverage passed 31 CA cases. A localhost/one-second stall-test setup expired before SSLRequest; diagnostics exposed initialization timeout, and literal loopback isolates the intended TLS-stage timeout/caller/Close/source-disposal cases. Final targeted suite passes 35 cases, including DER/PEM public sync/async/direct/source login and query, intermediate bundles, wrong-host/expired/untrusted/wrong-purpose rejection, pre-cancellation, lazy invalid-file failure, PEM/DER junk/private-key/PFX/UTF8/count/size limits, pooled file rotation and capacity recovery. Shared real TLS probes execute each exact packaged asset, including both Standard implementations on net8: valid trust, platform rejection, wrong CA/name, expiry, server EKU, intermediate bundle and IP SAN matching. Positive consumer probes intentionally end before AuthenticationOk and require EOF after encrypted StartupMessage; they prove TLS policy, not server identity or a full asset-level login.
- Review/fix: one fresh independent read-only review covered implementation, configuration, physical/pool ownership, tests and all-asset consumer wiring. It found one P2: duplicate basic-constraints/key-usage extensions could escape SingleOrDefault as InvalidOperationException and skip disposal of earlier bundle entries. Four malformed DER/bundle regressions reproduced the wrong exception before the fix. Explicit extension-count validation now rejects them as safe format errors, and an unexpected-failure cleanup guard disposes earlier entries; all 35 targeted cases then passed. Reviewer ran no tests/builds or second review; final fix verification is author evidence. No unresolved actionable important findings.

### E28 - DP11 bounded internal named PG prepared statements (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: development resumed after the E27 checkpoint. Q07 and V9-2 move to In progress for the internal protocol foundation; public KingbaseCommand.Prepare remains explicitly unsupported. This module establishes the named-statement dependency before public Command ownership/timeout/Reader integration. Automatic preparation and batch APIs remain Not started. - Source/contract: PgPreparedStatements adds physical-query-session-owned handles with exact SQL, ordered actual OIDs and reported standard_conforming_strings identity; mutable input signatures are snapshotted and parameter values/result schemas are not retained. PgExtendedQuery encodes named Parse/Sync, named Bind with unnamed portal/Describe/Execute/Sync, and statement Close/Sync. Require exact acknowledgments and ReadyForQuery; notices/parameter updates and bounded error drains retain existing semantics. Signature/owner/lexical/value/size errors reject locally. No interpolation, implicit retry or replay is introduced.
- Cache/ownership: retain at most 64 entries and 1 MiB of encoded SQL/name/OID signature storage. One session gate spans lookup, acknowledged LRU eviction and new Parse; wire exchanges retain the 1 MiB/4096-message inbound and 1 MiB outbound limits. Missing-statement Close may discard a stale entry; other synchronized Close errors, malformed/partial exchanges, interrupted I/O or uncertain post-Parse errors retire the session. SQLSTATE 26000/0A000 invalidates the affected handle without replay; explicit reprepare closes retained invalid entries. DISCARD ALL, DEALLOCATE ALL, reset and disposal revoke handles. A single DEALLOCATE revokes handles while retaining resource accounting until Close, because its tag omits the target name.

An aggregate-bound case initially hit the existing scalar value bound first; its CJK input was corrected to isolate aggregate framing, without changing product validation.

### E30 - Public explicit PG Prepare/Unprepare (2026-11-25)

- Implementation: KingbaseCommand exposes sync/async Prepare and Unprepare; Standard 2.0 has a typed PrepareAsync counterpart and newer assets override the BCL. Controls and execution share one operation pipeline for command/connection gates, total CommandTimeout/caller/Cancel budget, transaction observation, mode/encoding checks and safe server-exception mapping. Prepared execution binds fresh snapshots to the named physical statement and publishes the existing buffered Reader.
- Ownership: attach only a physical handle and logical generation. Require matching owner/generation/SQL/ordered actual OIDs/lexical state before named Bind. Values may change; incompatible signatures, edits, shared closure, eviction, reset or reopen detach before ordinary bound execution. Unprepare closes the attached shared handle; other commands then detach safely. A closed/stale owner detaches locally. Edits/disposal do no network I/O; retained resources stay under the existing 64-statement/1 MiB physical cache limits. No failed Execute is automatically replayed, including SQLSTATE 26000/0A000.

### E31 - Bounded automatic PG preparation (2026-11-25)

- Implementation/state: Q08 moves to In progress; DP11 remains open. Max Auto Prepare defaults to 0 (disabled), accepts 0..64, and limits automatic entries within the existing global 64-statement/1 MiB cache. Auto Prepare Min Usages defaults to 5 and accepts 1..Int32.MaxValue. Typed properties and normalized aliases participate in immutable physical configuration and pool identity. No SDK pin, package dependency or version change was introduced.
- Ownership/bounds: exact SQL, ordered actual OIDs and lexical status identify physical-session usage candidates across commands. Only successful unnamed exchanges advance usage; the threshold request prepares and executes a named statement with fresh values. Candidate LRU retains at most 128 entries/1 MiB of encoded SQL/signature bytes, with no values/schema. Automatic capacity closes its least recently used automatic entry before replacement; explicit Prepare promotes shared entries to explicit ownership. The global cache can still evict explicit handles. One query gate spans accounting/eviction/Parse/execution; one command deadline covers all exchanges, local frames/values validate before writes, and intermediate Close/Parse responses run the public mode/encoding guard. Managed transaction controls bypass automatic preparation.
- Invalidation: attached Unprepare, eviction and 26000/0A000 clear matching candidates; reset/DISCARD/DEALLOCATE clear all candidates. Unprepare before any handle is attached remains a no-op and retains candidate counts. Ordinary synchronized execution errors retain the selected handle for subsequent Unprepare; failed requests are never replayed. Disabled automatic preparation records no candidates and keeps detached commands on ordinary binding.
- Regression evidence: configuration and threshold tests were observed failing before implementation. Two intermediate-response mode-change cases failed before the additional guard. Targeted coverage includes candidate count/byte limits, threshold 1/high minimum, failed unnamed requests, fresh values, actual-OID/lexical identity, automatic LRU/explicit promotion, shared closure/reset, invalid plans, pre-I/O validation, concurrency/caller cancellation, public timeout/pool/transaction ownership and first-execution failure. Both public first-error attachment regressions failed before the review fix and passed afterward; the final targeted prepared/automatic selection passed 73 cases on net8. Logs: artifacts/validation/auto-prepare-{red,config-red,threshold-red,mode-red,mode-green,attachment-red,attachment-green}.log.

Two public regressions reproduced both paths. The author moved attachment before named execution and strengthened LIVE server-count/failed-transaction cases. No unresolved important finding remains; candidate-only Unprepare semantics are documented explicitly.

### E32 - PG DATE and lossless calendar values (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: T10 moves to In progress for the PG DATE input/result slice; DP05 remains open. Catalog-resolved base DATE of size 4 maps to Unspecified midnight DateTime, with KingbaseDate provider access and modern explicit DateOnly getters. KingbaseDate is inferred on all five assets and DateOnly on modern assets; DateTime inference stays unsupported until timestamp mappings exist. Explicit DateTime DATE input rejects a non-midnight value or UTC/Local Kind instead of truncating/converting it. Typed NULL, existing precision/Size validation, parameter snapshots, preparation and Reader ownership use the shared pipeline.
- Value/format contract: KingbaseDate is an immutable proleptic Gregorian date with signed historical years (negative = BC, no zero), canonical ISO text, explicit positive/negative infinity, equality and exact CLR conversion. Default is 0001-01-01. The seven-digit absolute year client envelope includes the exercised PG range; servers enforce their own actual range. BC leap validation translates to astronomical years. Default CLR conversion throws for BC/out-of-range/infinite values; the provider representation stays lossless without CLR sentinel substitutions. Non-NULL DATE output requires confirmed ISO, MDY/DMY/YMD and strict ISO grammar. Per-result DataRow format capture survives later ParameterStatus changes; mixed reported row formats revoke confirmation for that rowset. NULL/metadata require no date-text interpretation. No session setting is changed implicitly by the codec.

### E34 - DP05 PG TIMESTAMP/TIMESTAMPTZ calendar and UTC text slice (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: T10/DP05 remain In progress. Catalog-resolved base timestamp/timestamptz (size 8) have stable DateTime defaults with Unspecified/UTC Kind respectively. New immutable KingbaseTimestamp stores the lossless calendar/clock, and KingbaseTimestampTz stores its UTC calendar timestamp. Default values represent 0001-01-01 midnight; explicit positive/negative infinities remain separate from CLR sentinels. KingbaseDate's proleptic Gregorian/BC rules and seven-digit client envelope are shared, without promising that full envelope on the server. Finite clocks require exact microseconds and are below 24 hours; submicrosecond or noncanonical 24-hour input rejects. Non-CLR/infinite default conversion throws OverflowException; provider getters remain available.
- Input/result contract: Unspecified DateTime infers TIMESTAMP; UTC DateTime, DateTimeOffset and the instant provider infer TIMESTAMPTZ; Local DateTime rejects. Explicit binding enforces these same Kind/type rules. DateTimeOffset normalizes its supplied offset to UTC, without a machine timezone or DST choice. DbType.DateTime/DateTime2 selects TIMESTAMP and DateTimeOffset selects TIMESTAMPTZ; appended provider enum members preserve earlier values. Explicit DATE semantics remain unchanged. ISO timestamp text has one date/clock separator and optional final uppercase BC; TIMESTAMPTZ requires a numeric whole-second offset below 16 hours in magnitude and formats canonically with +00. UTC normalization advances/retreats at most one civil day and skips historical year zero. Results reuse immutable ISO DateStyle confirmation; numeric offsets contain each row's zone fact, so later TimeZone updates do not reinterpret buffered instants. DateTimeOffset Reader access is explicit for TIMESTAMPTZ and returns zero offset. Parser/conversion errors omit payload/inner exception details.

### E36 - DP11 public sequential PG batch APIs (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: Q09 moves to In progress. KingbaseBatch, KingbaseBatchCommand and KingbaseBatchCommandCollection derive from DbBatch counterparts on modern assets and expose the same provider-specific contract on both Standard assets. Connection and factory expose batch creation. Each command owns parameters, Text SQL, RecordsAffected and ExecutionStatus; collections reject duplicate/foreign ownership and cap statement count at 256. Timeout follows the connection unless overridden, including zero/unlimited. The batch uses the existing internal Command runner so one connection gate, total deadline, cancellation/Cancel/Close/disposal and Reader ownership cover the complete sequence.
- Execution/reporting: execute separate synchronized exchanges in order using current lexical status, actual catalog OIDs, per-statement input snapshots and shared explicit/automatic preparation. Stop at the first error without implicit transaction, retry or continuation. FailedCommandIndex identifies the zero-based stop location and KingbaseException.BatchCommand identifies a rejected command through typed/BCL access. Status is NotExecuted, Succeeded, Failed or OutcomeUnknown; acknowledgment describes execution, not transaction commit. Prior successes/counts remain visible after later failure. Preparation preserves execution status; each non-reentrant Execute resets all statuses/counts before preflight validation. Aggregate buffered results cap 256 results, 1024 rows, 1024 columns and 1 MiB text/metadata, with checked Int32 affected totals; acknowledged capacity/count failures stop before another command. Reader visibility flags do not skip execution. ExecuteScalar validates its selected scalar before later SQL and otherwise executes the full sequence.
- Regression evidence: the initial BCL CanCreateBatch assertion failed with false before implementation. The first expanded wire run passed 21 cases and reproduced a delayed aggregate affected-count overflow that allowed later statements to execute; moving the check between statements fixes it. A preparation-disconnect regression failed with OutcomeUnknown before distinguishing execution frame writes from Parse/Close controls. The corrected Parse/Sync peer reproduced RED before the fix; all 31 then passed. A retained-handle disposal case failed before detaching batch command handles under the runner gate and guarding asynchronous attachments. The final focused batch selection passed 34 net8 cases covering ordered parameters/results, sync/async/BCL APIs, collection bounds/ownership, first/middle/last server failure, per-statement local rejection, current lexical status after SET, cancellation/Cancel/Close/disposal, one total timeout, explicit preparation/fresh values, repeated Execute reset, aggregate row/column/byte/count limits, visibility flags and acknowledgment retention for unsupported types/identity changes. Logs: artifacts/validation/sequential-batch-{red,green,wire-first,wire-green,prepare-outcome-red,prepare-outcome-green,dispose-red,dispose-green}.log. ### E48 - DP13 conservative PG Reader KeyInfo (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: M06/Q16/DP06/DP11/DP13 remain In progress. KeyInfo works for sync/async PG commands, explicit preparation and sequential batches, including nonexecuting SchemaOnly. Both schema APIs share visible base identities/aliases and conservative direct-table nullability/key/unique/identity/owned-nextval/structural-read-only facts. Complete immediate non-null distinct-slot keys are required; joins/views/SRFs/complex shapes stay unknown, ordinary inheritance requires ONLY, and no hidden columns are added. Generated/ALWAYS identity read-only is structural, not a privilege guarantee. Snapshot-isolated catalog reads suppress base names/guarantees. Parent feature rows stay open; ledger remains Complete 1 / In progress 63 / Not started 47 / Deferred 11 (122 total).
- Consistency/bounds: discovery Describe -> catalog-before -> original operation -> catalog-after, with authoritative Describe replacing execution under SchemaOnly. Compare descriptors, published facts and attribute/default/dependency/table/namespace/index witnesses; reject observed drift without SQL replay. Retain original acknowledged batch status/counts; preflight-only cancellation/failure stays NotExecuted. Catalog names/versions count toward per-command and aggregate batch budgets; table signatures are retained once per table. This bounded guard does not establish unconditional concurrent-DDL snapshot consistency.
- Focused evidence: the initial public KeyInfo cases failed NotSupportedException. Expanded loopback/parser tests cover typed bound catalog identities, malformed/contradictory/restricted facts, unknown attributes, drift/no-retry, cancellation, batch acknowledgment/phase and aggregate bytes. Independent review found sorting-tail SRFs, same-named nextval, repeated index slots and repeated wide-shape work; corrections require clause boundaries, actual built-in function dependency identity, conservative repeated-slot exclusion and one set-based shape check per result. Three new parser regressions failed before the boundary fix; LIVE SRF/shadow-function/repeated-index regressions failed before correction. A shadowed regclass type also reproduced a lookup failure before fully qualifying its function-signature type. Focused offline selection passed 54 cases before this final qualification-only correction (artifacts/validation/e48-final-focused.log); final full verification follows below.

### E51 - DP13 PG DataAdapter batching (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: support nonnegative UpdateBatchSize (default 1; 0 stages all eligible rows) through BCL batching hooks and the existing KingbaseBatch engine. Snapshot SQL/type/parameters and mutable bytes after RowUpdating under command ownership; reject invalid source commands on their own row. Validate one connection/transaction/timeout before dispatch, split logical batches into at most 256-command physical groups and share a monotonic timeout across them. Logical queue memory follows the requested batch size; existing physical input/result bounds remain. No implicit transaction, pipelining or replay. M08/Q09 and the whole-requirement inventory remain open: Complete 1 / Implemented 3 / In progress 61 / Not started 46 / Deferred 11, total 122.
- Failure/event contracts: preserve positively acknowledged writes after later timeout/disconnect/local validation, and keep failed, unknown-count/outcome and unsent rows pending with distinct errors. Zero-row UPDATE/DELETE retains BCL concurrency handling. A fully acknowledged batch can surface a later diagnostic without losing acknowledgments; RowUpdated can suppress or replace it. Successful SkipAllRemainingRows clears once, honors handler acceptance and preserves the next row's previous RowError and parameter values. Reentrant Update and mid-update size edits reject. FirstReturnedRecord rejects for batching; use UpdatedRowSource.None. Transaction acknowledgment and commit remain separate.
- Regression/review evidence: configuration cases, partial-ack wire failures, missing/disposed row commands, successful SkipAll, diagnostic suppression/replacement and stopped-row state were observed failing before their fixes. Final focused runs pass 11 protocol cases, 8 adapter unit cases and 24 LIVE batch cases. Independent read-only review traced BCL queue cleanup, acknowledgment publication, diagnostic handlers and stopped-row restoration and reports no remaining actionable finding; the reviewer did not independently run tests. Temporary BCL reference decompilation and raw LIVE evidence remain outside the repository.

### E52 - DP06 four-mode PG-wire public text execution (2026-11-25)

- Scope/state: public unpooled Connection/DataSource text execution now accepts detected or explicit Oracle/MySQL/SQL Server compatibility modes alongside PG, reusing actual identity/catalog OIDs and the existing scalar/Reader/parameter/preparation/batch engine. Explicit non-PG Pooling=true rejects; Auto pooled discovery retains the existing reset guard. Non-PG GetSchema/KeyInfo/generated writes and transaction chaining remain unsupported. - Lexical contract: recognize Oracle q/nq paired/custom literal delimiters, SQL Server bracket quoting/doubled closing delimiters, MySQL backticks/hash comments and quoted variables before PG operator scanning. Bare @name/:name are provider markers, while @@ and quoted MySQL variable expressions remain server-owned. Require confirmed standard_conforming_strings. Reject MySQL executable comments and backslashes in SQL bodies delimited by single quotes, double quotes or backticks, including prefixed strings/variables, until sql_mode interactions are confirmed. Bound parameter values may contain backslashes; dollar-quoted bodies retain exact literal contents without escape interpretation. Preserve one-statement/size/malformed-body/unused-slot checks and no SQL translation or replay.
- Review fixes: preserve PG metadata provenance in every builder-generated command and adapter batch snapshot; direct execution, Prepare and physical batch dispatch reject non-PG execution even after connection reuse. Nine real temporary-fixture direct/per-row/batched regressions failed before this fix. Let Idle end ordinary transaction ownership; only recognized PG AND CHAIN replaces an active transaction handle. Wire regressions for an active-T COMMIT/ROLLBACK failed before the correction; the real SQL Server savepoint spelling is accepted and now verified to retain the handle and undo writes. Correct stale END WORK/ABORT unit expectations. Add zero-parameter executable-comment/escape tests to avoid false positives from unused parameters. MySQL quoted-variable/operator-delimiter/lowercase executable-comment and four ambiguous string-body failures have observed RED evidence. Logs: artifacts/validation/e52-{planner-red,review-red,operator-red,mysql-escape-red}.log and external e52-cached-builder-red.

Public tests cover explicit-mode scalar/text/typed NULL/binary/numeric inputs, preparation with fresh values, SchemaOnly, Reader ownership, synchronized-error recovery, detected-mode dialect bodies, sequential temporary-table batches and explicit rollback. SQL Server additionally exercises ROLLBACK TRANSACTION savepoint_name. MySQL division by zero returns NULL; SQL Server count(*) returns Int32 and an uncast string literal may use an unsupported type, so the supported lexer fixture explicitly casts pg_catalog.text. A private external probe confirms MySQL no-space -- comments, an independent sql_mode setting, rejection of the probed NO_BACKSLASH_ESCAPES assignment, and server rejection of the probed uninitialized quoted variable (42703). Both queries reserve every observed OID, including omitted types, so contradictory identities fail. Native query names use qualified nameeq predicates compatible with SQL Server mode. A matching ordinary synchronized Idle rejection in the bounded permission/object/unsupported allowlist leaves proven PG mappings usable; fatal/uncertain, malformed, duplicate and mode-drift responses fail initialization. Native OIDs refresh during pool reset before publication.
- Public/value contract: append ten selectors without renumbering earlier values. Keep uint and bare DbType.UInt32 as OID; explicit UnsignedInteger selects numeric uint4 or lossless int8 fallback. UInt64 selects uint8 or actual int16; signed128 uses BigInteger on all five assets with exact [-2^127, 2^127-1] and bounded text parsing. SByte/Byte use proven native tiny types or int2 (MySQL Byte uses uint4). Preserve exact logical input widths and typed-NULL conflicts before application I/O. Default CLR follows physical codec; explicit uint/ulong getters range-check signed-wide fallbacks. SQL Server BIT uses 0/1 Boolean, never MySQL multibit semantics. Strings preserve Unicode and padding; client Size does not emulate server byte/national-character modifiers.

### E56 - DP05/DP08 native temporal types, calendars and YEAR (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: eleven append-only selectors cover Oracle DATE; SQL Server DATE/DATETIME/DATETIME2/SMALLDATETIME/TIME; and MySQL DATE/DATETIME/TIMESTAMP/TIME/YEAR. Native type names, layouts, I/O identities, mode and actual base OIDs select codecs. Bare DbType/DateTime/TimeSpan inference stays PG; new MySQL provider structs infer their own families. T11-T14/T16/T31 move from Not started to In progress. Whole-parent inventory: Complete 1 / Implemented 3 / In progress 70 / Not started 37 / Deferred 11, total 122; no completion percentage is inferred.
- Catalog/input contract: extend the existing bounded protected sys query; require verified base layouts or the two explicit trusted domains (Oracle sys.date over actual PG timestamp, MySQL sys.year over actual PG int4), including matching scalar base category/size. Do not accept generic domains, copied OIDs or same-name other-mode aliases. Oracle DATE preserves whole-second time with DateTime/KingbaseTimestamp and rejects client fraction truncation. SQL Server DATE enforces finite years 1–9999 on both default/provider paths; DATETIME/SMALLDATETIME require exact milliseconds, DATETIME2/TIME retain seven fractional digits. Unspecified Kind is required, without local/UTC/fixed-offset conversion. DateOnly read/write is symmetric on modern assets. Existing Size and numeric precision/scale reject temporal inputs.
- Provider/result contract: immutable KingbaseMySqlDate/KingbaseMySqlDateTime preserve bounded zero/partial/invalid calendar components and seven-digit years, exact microseconds, invariant bounded parsing, equality/hash and explicit CLR overflow. Default DateTime conversion never invents MinValue; provider access remains available afterward. MySQL TIMESTAMP is the server's displayed wall clock, not an inferred UTC instant. TIME is signed TimeSpan through ±838:59:59 with exact microseconds; negative/long values reject TimeOnly. YEAR uses exact int input and explicit zero semantics, with actual server domain checks. Native calendar parsing requires the result's captured ISO DateStyle; SQL text fractions require ASCII digits after the separator. Oracle raw BC/infinity and DateStyle behavior are independently characterized; full binary/range/profile acceptance remains separate.
- RED/review/portability evidence: 21 initial native protocol, seven provider-existence/value and eleven prepared LIVE cases fail before implementation. Expanded provider suite has 21 cases; final native protocol suite has 36 and native/scalar connected selection 109 passes. Seven review regressions fail with 29 controls passing, then pass after symmetric DateOnly input, strict fraction grammar and SQL Server DATE provider-range fixes. Independent isolated server probes confirm invalid SQL Server DATE ranges are rejected and boundary years 1/9999 accepted. Reviewer confirms no remaining actionable issue, after source/test/package-fixture inspection without an independent test run. Initial Standard builds expose record-generated IsExternalInit; replace the internal descriptor with a plain readonly struct and rerun all gates without adding a dependency. Logs: artifacts/validation/e56-{native-red,providers-red,native-green,providers-full-green,native-boundaries-red,review-red,review-green,scalars-connected-green}.log.

The supplied MySQL server rejects tested zero/partial/invalid dates with 22008 and YEAR zero with 23514, including independently probed empty sql_mode; this is explicit rejection evidence, not successful zero-value acceptance. ### E57 - DP12 notifications and resumable Wait (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: implement public Notification with immutable ProcessId/Channel/Payload, synchronous/asynchronous bool Wait with int/TimeSpan timeouts and caller cancellation on PG wire in all four tested modes. Dispatch only during commands or explicit waits; no background push, replay or automatic re-LISTEN. B06 moves to Implemented with broader applicable acceptance pending. Whole-parent inventory: Complete 1 / Implemented 4 / In progress 70 / Not started 36 / Deferred 11, total 122; these are closure states, not a development percentage.
- Ownership/error contract: strict positive PID and exact UTF8 channel/payload parsing; reject malformed/trailing data. Bind the sink to the current logical operation/generation, validate mode/encoding before callbacks, reject same-connection command/schema/Wait reentry, and detach before Reader publication/close/reset. Initialization and unowned/reset traffic parse and discard notifications. Capture handler exceptions, drain query acknowledgments and validate results/metadata before throwing; server/protocol/schema errors and confirmed rollback take precedence. Preserve acknowledged batch outcomes and clear a spurious failed-command index. Failed BEGIN handlers trigger independently bounded transaction cleanup, closing on cleanup failure.
- Resumption contract: idle-only Wait sends no SQL and holds the normal operation gate. -1ms is infinite, zero polls; finite TimeSpan inputs require exact Int32 milliseconds. A monotonic deadline spans nonnotification traffic. Timeout/caller cancellation preserves one bounded whole-frame read, including partial header/body; the next Wait or command consumes it under its own aggregate budget. Keep an independent 1MiB retained payload cap, honor the timeout winner/deadline and observe pending task faults. Ordinary query cancellation still retires. Close revokes the sink and aborts pending I/O. This is not a continuously running receive pump.
- RED/review evidence: eight initial protocol and four initial LIVE cases fail before implementation. Independent read-only review identifies BEGIN cleanup, definite rollback precedence, final KeyInfo callback/error ordering, late frame consumption and stale byte-budget defects; five regressions fail with 23 controls passing before fixes. Connected review selection passes 210 cases, then the final timer accounting correction is covered by complete verification. Additional generation-scope and acknowledged-batch regressions protect stale failures and partial-success reporting. Reviewer reports no remaining actionable findings after source/test inspection, without independently running tests. Logs: artifacts/validation/e57-notifications-red.log, e57-review-red.log and e57-review-final-green.log; raw LIVE/probe/configuration data stays outside the repository.

A notice alone does not make Wait return true; its handler failure ends idle waiting immediately after the complete frame and propagates without retiring a healthy session. ### E59 - DP12 streaming text and raw binary COPY (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: B01/B03 move from Not started to Implemented with applicable profile/platform acceptance pending. Public sync/async text import/export and KingbaseCopyStream support one client COPY statement, actual direction/format/column facts, strict UTF8, opaque binary bytes, UInt64 acknowledged counts and exclusive connection ownership in the four tested PG-wire modes. Text Dispose completes; raw import requires explicit Complete; unfinished raw import cancels and unfinished export retires. Typed binary row B02 and all LOB work remain separate. Whole-parent inventory: Complete 1 / Implemented 6 / In progress 70 / Not started 34 / Deferred 11, total 122; no development percentage is inferred.
- Data/protocol contract: stream CopyData directly across partial reads without the buffered query's aggregate 1MiB data cap; writes and modern adapters use bounded chunks. Retain the 64MiB frame-length cap and a separate whole-exchange 4096-frame/1MiB control/empty-message budget. Reuse the actual-mode SQL lexer and a top-level token consumer to reject files/PROGRAM and known text-direction mismatch before Q. Validate G/H format/count arrays, d/c/C/Z phases and exact COPY count. Preserve E between C/Z, independently reported transaction state and original server/receive errors. An import receive task consumes notices/settings/notifications/early errors while writes proceed, preventing duplex backpressure deadlock; it owns only that explicit COPY exchange. No notification service is added outside COPY.
- Ownership/closure contract: a generation-bound logical marker and query gate remain held until terminal acknowledgment or retirement. COPY resources, commands/schema/Wait and transaction completion reject reentry/overlap. Atomically validate marker/session/generation before abort and detach per-call registrations before release; stale resources cannot damage a new command. Reject overlapping cancellation/disposal while completion is unacknowledged rather than asserting cancellation success. Guard raw/text wrapper disposal before changing state. TextReader owns its copy separately from its leave-open StreamReader, so rejected disposal preserves its pending read and cleanup path. Callback errors wait for terminal synchronization and never erase server/protocol errors or acknowledged counts.
- Cancellation/deadline contract: Command Timeout spans the COPY lifetime including application pauses, with Begin's token bound to that lifetime. Pre-canceled calls do no I/O. In-flight cancellation/Close retires. Import Cancel stops the application timer and uses independent Cleanup Timeout; distinguish the actual timeout source. A received E and terminal request are ordered under one lock: never send f or swallow a known earlier server cancellation while waiting for its Z. Consume 57014 only after sending this operation's CopyFail. Known receive faults remain visible. Cancellation is not outer Rollback; all observed outer cancellations return Ready=E and require explicit application rollback. Deadlines bound I/O, not synchronous application handlers.

A controlled duplex stream deterministically fails when the receive path is disabled, then succeeds while every write waits for its Notice to be read. Seven old-behavior regressions reproduce stale abort, cancellation callback loss, pre-cancellation and C/E/Z precedence defects; subsequent review rounds produce 5, 3, 2 and 1 failing regressions for disposal/cleanup/error-order/unknown-completion/text-reader ownership boundaries. Independent cleanup timeout classification also fails before its fix. Final connected selection passes 232 cases and the complete verifier includes 66 new COPY cases. ### E60 - DP12 transactional OID large-object manager and streams (2026-11-26)

- Scope: public KingbaseLargeObjectManager Create/Open/OpenRead/OpenReadWrite/Unlink sync/async and KingbaseLargeObjectStream byte-array reads/writes, cursor/Length, 64-bit Seek and SetLength, Flush and bounded close. Actual catalog-bound oid/int4/int8/bytea values call qualified pg_catalog functions on four PG-wire modes. B04 becomes Implemented with applicability acceptance pending; B05 ordinary BLOB/CLOB/NCLOB remains separate and Not started.
- Ownership: require a healthy explicit local transaction. One logical command marker and monotonic Command Timeout cover every statement in a public operation, including partial writes and Length cursor restoration. Maximum byte-array transfer chunk is 64KiB; inherited BCL Span/Memory adapters can stage the full caller buffer and have no bounded-memory promise. No whole-object buffering, implicit transaction/commit, file-transfer API or locator emulation is added.
- Descriptor lifecycle: bind handles to logical transaction, physical session/generation and sequence. Public savepoints retain exact scope marks; rollback invalidates reverted children, including released nested scopes, without closing a reused fd. Raw scope controls clear untrusted marks; raw rollback invalidates conservatively. Commit/rollback/close/pool reset invalidate old handles. Unlink invalidates matching OID handles without a second server close. Atomically choose immediate close, failed-transaction deferral or stale forget; recovered deferred closes use Cleanup Timeout without user callbacks. Transaction end releases remaining descriptors.
- Errors and review: validate exact scalar metadata/type/counts and acknowledged positions. Invalid/uncertain results or interrupted I/O retire without replay; pre-cancellation and no-I/O cancellation preserve the transaction. Deferred callback failures follow cursor/state updates; failed Create/Open publication cleans up under an independent deadline or retires. Preserve primary SQLSTATE over secondary Broken StateChange exceptions, including raw command/batch controls and failed batch identity. Independent review found raw-savepoint journal, stale-generation, uncertain-result, failed-Length-restore, publication cancellation, callback precedence and deferred-close issues; targeted failing/passing regressions cover the reproducible paths. The final read-only reviewer reported no remaining important findings, without independently running tests or accessing LIVE. The atomic close decision was verified by source review and existing lifecycle regressions. Logs: artifacts/validation/e60-{api-red,review-red,review2-red,review3-red,review4-red,review4-green}.log.
- Real characterization and acceptance: independent external probes confirmed all qualified functions, access flags, unlink closing existing descriptors, savepoint parent survival/child invalidation with immediate fd reuse, and seek beyond 2GiB without writing/allocating 2GiB.
