### E64 - Bounded streaming parameter input (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: accept caller-owned Stream for explicit bytea/BLOB and TextReader for explicit text/CLOB/NCLOB, including base Binary/String DbType in all four PG-wire modes. Execute at the current position without closing, seeking, replaying or truncating the source. Validate SQL, every type/scalar and duplicate input references before reading. Prepare/SchemaOnly do not consume sources. Size bounds raw bytes or UTF16 units; strict incremental UTF8 preserves BOM data, carries split surrogate pairs and rejects NUL/malformed Unicode. Ordinary scalar/array/string limits stay unchanged; B05 remains In progress and no parent closure count changes.
- Resource/execution contract: PG length prefixes require one exclusively opened CreateNew/DeleteOnClose temporary Bind body. Fixed source/encoding/send buffers bound managed working memory; the entire Bind frame is capped at 64MiB including lengths, names and binary hex expansion. Stage once before automatic preparation; share the request across buffered/sequential execution and preserve E/Q execution-write accounting. Dispose the spool and retained input references after send or failure. Queued DataAdapter snapshots reject streams/readers because row snapshots cannot independently own them. Windows exclusive sharing and modern Unix user-only creation are implemented; Standard assets depend on OS temporary-directory policy.
- Cancellation/ownership: command token, Cancel, Close and deadline race isolated caller-read tasks. Late reads cannot reach the spool or wire; faults are observed and buffers cleared. Arbitrary caller code cannot be terminated or exposed as a reusable pending-read handle: the owner must independently establish read completion before reusing a canceled source. Local staging failure preserves an untouched session; partial transmission retires it. Lazy batch failure retains earlier acknowledgments and leaves later input unread, with independent bounded Reader cleanup.
- Regression/review: initial nonseekable binary/text input, queued-adapter and disposed-request cases were observed failing before their corresponding corrections. Final tests add 32 protocol cases covering large input, preparation/nonconsumption, strict encoding/Size, duplicate references/preflight, current position/empty/BOM, all cancellation sources in buffered/sequential execution, exact frame/name bounds, no replay, spool ownership and send-failure accounting. ### E66 - Exact numeric and PG temporal binary COPY (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: extend the E65 typed row owner with actual pg_catalog numeric/date/time/timetz/timestamp/timestamptz/interval base types, including catalog kind/base/element/storage-width checks. No new public wrapper or transport. Numeric accepts Decimal/KingbaseNumeric and returns KingbaseNumeric with exact coefficient/display scale. Temporal inputs reuse exact provider/CLR validators; output is KingbaseDate, TimeSpan, KingbaseTimeTz, KingbaseTimestamp, UTC KingbaseTimestampTz or KingbaseInterval. Modern DateOnly/TimeOnly work through the modern assets. All five TFMs and 0.1.0/dependencies remain unchanged. B02 remains In progress; parent inventory remains Complete1, Implemented8, In progress71, Not started31, Deferred11, total122.
- Bounds/correctness: numeric base10000 count/weight/sign/scale/digits are validated before bounded BigInteger arithmetic; redundant zero groups do not consume coefficient precision. Preserve the provider's 16384 coefficient digits and binary scale0–16383; provider scale16384 rejects locally. Never round hidden fractional precision. DATE/timestamp Gregorian epoch conversions skip historical year zero and floor negative remainders; enforce PG finite ranges and explicit infinities. TIMETZ converts seconds-west to provider east offsets. INTERVAL preserves independent months/days/int64 microseconds. Actual ParameterStatus integer_datetimes=on is required for clock/timestamp/interval selectors before SQL dispatch, including NULL-only paths; missing/off/unknown rejects without occupying the connection. DATE/numeric do not require it. Existing row budgets, unsent validation, lifecycle/callback gates and unfinished-export retirement remain in force.
- Test-first evidence: 48 new codec cases failed against missing implementations before passing; missing integer-datetime preflight cases failed by dispatching unsupported COPY before the local gate was added. Expanded tests include literal formats, independent CLR calendar comparisons, actual catalog layouts, fragmentation, decimal/provider precision and scale, bounded significant arithmetic with 65535 redundant groups, malformed fields, BC/infinities/range extrema and unsent-invalid-row recovery. Numeric assertions check Coefficient and Scale separately because provider numerical equality permits equivalent scales. An unprivileged full protocol run hit 38 certificate private-key access failures; final gates run with the required local certificate-store access, without treating that sandbox limitation as a product defect.
- Four-mode server observations: independent send-function literals establish epoch/era/time/offset/interval and finite calendar extrema. Numeric infinities are server-rejected (22P02 text,22P03 binary), while NaN is accepted in binary. SQL Server rejects text NaN and displays binary NaN as text zero, but binary export retains NaN. A tested extreme interval is accepted and retained in binary while server text formatting rejects22008. MySQL BC timestamptz text can omit its offset; strict text parsing continues to reject that ambiguous form, and independent verification uses explicit UTC projection. Initial failed fixture assumptions and final passing LIVE evidence remain external. These scoped server limits are not codec acceptance for other profiles.

### E67 - JSON/JSONB and PG bit/varbit binary COPY (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: extend the existing typed COPY codecs to 23 actual catalog-bound PG base types. JSON/JSONB use exact String inputs/outputs, strict UTF8 and no literal NUL; JSONB requires version byte 1. Syntax and JSONB normalization remain server-owned. PG bit/varbit use BitArray, a network int32 bit count and MSB-first bytes, preserving leading zeros, empty/NULL and non-byte-aligned values. Retain the existing 1048576-bit client bound and owned input snapshots. Validate count/length with overflow-safe arithmetic before allocation; malformed padding rejects. A valid oversized bit result throws OverflowException and retires an unfinished export. No public API/dependency/version/TFM changes. B02 and parent inventory remain unchanged: Complete1, Implemented8, In progress71, Not started31, Deferred11, total122.
- Test-first/diagnosis: initial 57 codec/catalog cases show 37 missing-support failures before implementation; final targeted/full verification is recorded below. Additional public cases cover literal fragmentation, invalid rows left unsent, mutable bits, malformed later rows and oversized output retirement. The first four-mode LIVE run passes 19/20; independent COUNT probes establish SQL Server mode returns Int32 while the other modes return Int64, both zero after rollback. The fixture now checks the lossless count value instead of assuming a single CLR type; no driver coercion change. Correct the peer fixture's independent SELECT OID to match its declared catalog. Independent review also finds padding validation must precede the oversized-valid-value ceiling; four new classification failures are observed before the one-line ordering fix, then six related cases pass. Public output tests confirm both errors retire ownership after a prior row. Private evidence remains external.
- Source-built LIVE: all 445 cases pass on each net8/net9/net10, 1,335 executions with zero failures/skips; raw e67-full-live logs/TRX remain external. Twenty new LIVE cases cover independent send functions/server-generated exports, imported Unicode/exact JSON versus normalized JSONB, fixed/varying/empty/NULL bits, server 22P02 syntax rejection/recovery, maximum client bit count, explicit rollback and two same-PID pooled borrowers/stale disposal. The corrected focused net10 LIVE run passes 20/20 with zero failures/skips. Exact archive probes add all four types and NULL to the existing fragmented rows/two borrowers/four simulated modes.

### E68 - XML binary COPY with Unicode encoding normalization (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: extend typed COPY to 24 actual catalog-bound PG base types with pg_catalog.xml. String input removes one leading U+FEFF and the encoding attribute only from a complete syntactically valid XML declaration. Preserve version, standalone, remaining whitespace and body; malformed declarations stay unchanged for server validation. Strict UTF8/no literal NUL, SQL NULL versus empty, existing row limits and lifetime/ownership remain enforced. The client does not parse XML bodies or resolve DTDs/entities. Compute retained UTF8 size before allocating one byte array, and encode the retained segments without normalized string copies. No public API, dependencies, version or TFM changes. T20/B02 remain In progress; the 122-parent inventory remains Complete1, Implemented8, In progress71, Not started31, Deferred11.
- Test-first/review correction: initial 38 codec/catalog/public cases show 33 missing-support failures and five catalog-rejection controls. The final XML subset has 44 cases; all 277 binary COPY cases pass in the focused net10 run. Cover exact Unicode/declaration/BOM literals, both quote styles, malformed declarations, body/entity preservation, normalized budgets, qualified catalog layout, invalid rows left unsent, fragmented import/export and malformed later rows retiring unfinished output. Independent review identifies oversized normalized-string copies before the budget check. Three 8MiB allocation regressions fail before direct-segment encoding and pass afterward, together with double-BOM/malformed-standalone/truncated-declaration controls. No unresolved source review finding remains.
- Source-built LIVE: all 461 cases pass on each net8/net9/net10, 1,383 executions with zero failures/skips; raw e68-final-live logs/TRX stay external. Four new scenarios in each mode (16 cases) independently check xml_send hex, SQL-generated binary export, typed imports against ordinary text Readers/send/export, Unicode with UTF16/Latin1/unknown declaration metadata, BOM, standalone, empty/NULL/fragments, server syntax rejection without partial import, explicit rollback and two same-PID pooled borrowers with stale disposal. The tested servers normalize output declarations; Oracle empty SQL string literal is NULL while binary empty XML remains String. Invalid XML body reports 2200N; the tested malformed UTF16 declaration reports 22023. Private raw discovery identifies encoding-conversion/BOM limitations but does not establish a general raw XML encoding contract.
- Validation correction: full regression exposes an existing SCRAM cancellation test whose thread-pool continuation can cancel only after CPU derivation finishes. Isolated reproduction passes while the original full run fails. The fixture now starts derivation on a dedicated worker and cancels synchronously after its start signal; original-token, cancellation-bound and terminal-authentication assertions remain. All 15 focused net8 lifetime tests pass. Independent review accepts this fixture-only change; it remains timing-based cancellation coverage rather than an instrumented proof of a particular loop checkpoint. Product authentication code is unchanged.

### E69 - Catalog and identifier scalar types (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: ordinary catalog queries now work in all four PG-wire modes. Actual catalog resolution adds `pg_catalog.name` (size 64) and the internal 1-byte `pg_catalog."char"` as `String`, the `regproc`/`regprocedure`/`regoper`/`regoperator`/`regclass`/`regtype`/`regconfig`/`regdictionary`/`regnamespace`/`regrole` OID aliases (size 4) as their server text form, and `xid`/`cid` (size 4) as `UInt32` using OID's nonnegative exact-text rules. The internal `"char"` requires one ASCII character or the empty string, the latter being its zero byte. Before E69, `SELECT current_user`, `SELECT nspname FROM pg_catalog.pg_namespace`, `SELECT relname FROM pg_catalog.pg_class` and the `name`-typed `information_schema` columns all failed with "no scalar text codec"; this was a real-server blocker, not an acceptance gap. No public API, dependency, version or TFM changes.
- Root cause/ruling: the scalar guard rejected every type whose `typelem` was nonzero. The tested V9 servers report `pg_catalog.name` with element type `pg_catalog."char"` (`typelem=18`, `typarray=1003`), so that guard was factually wrong for this product. Array-ness is now identified by catalog category `A`, which also covers `int2vector`/`oidvector`; kind, domain/base, size and the existing per-codec category guards remain mandatory. `"char"` maps to `String` rather than `Char` so `GetString` keeps working for `relkind`/`prokind`/`typdelim`; `GetChar` therefore retains the reader's exact-CLR rule and throws. Identifier inputs need an explicit `DataTypeName` because a bare SQL NULL cannot infer an identifier type.
- Test-first correction: 29 offline cases (decode/encode/streaming round trips, NULL versus empty, `"char"` length and ASCII bounds, `xid`/`cid` sign and overflow, the Kingbase `name`-with-`char`-element layout, category-`A` rejection, reader metadata and typed access) and 20 four-mode LIVE cases. Three existing offline tests had encoded the superseded `typelem`-only contract; they now mutate category `A` instead, and one new control asserts a non-array scalar keeps its codec with a nonzero element type. The catalog test now derives its expected query from the production name list instead of duplicating it.
- Independent review correction: the first submission rejected the empty text of `"char"`. That was wrong: PG renders the zero byte as the empty string, and `pg_attribute.attidentity`/`attgenerated` hold exactly that value, so `SELECT attname, attidentity FROM pg_catalog.pg_attribute` still failed on all four modes (reproduced before the fix on every mode). Validation now accepts the empty string and one ASCII character, rejects two characters or non-ASCII, and the offline and LIVE cases assert `""` instead of rejection. The same review found that `DataTypeName` plus a compatible `DbType` threw for every added codec because the compatibility check required a provider enum member; catalog/identifier codecs now map to the provider family with the same CLR shape (text family, or OID-style UInt32), with offline and LIVE coverage for compatible and conflicting constraints. Review also confirmed that no path can now decode a true array as a scalar and that `xid`/`cid` remain exact. Two review notes are recorded rather than implemented: binary COPY and native discovery keep a zero-element-type precondition because their selectors are base scalars (now commented), and streaming `GetChars`/`GetTextReader` retain the pre-existing bypass of buffered validation.
- Source-built LIVE against the user's four-mode V9 installation: 481 cases pass on each net8/net9/net10 (1,443 executions, zero failures/skips), including the 20 new cases. Verified per mode: `current_user`/`current_schema` as `name`; `pg_class` `relname`/`relkind`/`xmin`/`cid`; `pg_database`, `pg_namespace`, `pg_proc` and `pg_type` identifier columns; `regclass`/`regproc`/`regtype`/`regnamespace` casts; typed `name`/`char`/`xid` parameters including prepared, NULL and locally rejected `"vv"`/empty input with the session still usable; streaming `name` columns with NULL preserved; `information_schema.parameters` metadata. One earlier full run lost four MySQL-mode sessions to `EndOfStreamException` under parallel load; two subsequent full three-host runs passed completely, so this is recorded as endpoint load behaviour rather than a functional regression and remains an open robustness item.
- Final offline/package gate: ./eng/verify.ps1 -ContinuousIntegration exits 0 with 2,664 protocol +497 unit cases per net8/net9/net10 (9,483 executions), 522 expected offline LIVE-method skips, five-TFM core/DI builds with zero warnings/errors and all five fresh-cache exact-archive consumers passing. dotnet format analyzers --verify-no-changes --severity warn --no-restore exits 0 for the core project across all five targets. Evidence: artifacts/verify-e69-final.log, artifacts/analyzers-e69-final.log. NuGetAudit=false retains the fresh online vulnerability-audit limitation.
- Remaining scope: MySQL-mode `information_schema.routines` still needs enum/`SET` codecs (T23) before its `routine_type`/`security_type` columns are readable end to end; `int2vector`/`oidvector`, `money`, network/geometry/full-text, range/array/composite and remaining identifier families are unimplemented. T30 remains In progress. No push, tag or publication.






### E70 - Catalog enum and SET text values (2026-11-26)

- Scope/state: enum and SET columns now read as `String` in all four PG-wire modes. The catalog query keeps its fixed built-in name filter and additionally selects every row of kind `e` (category `E`) or `s` (category `M`), so user-named label types need no name list. Dynamic rows must have a positive length and no base or element type; inconsistent dynamic rows are skipped, unknown kinds still retire the session, and the fixed rows keep their single-namespace integrity check. The server remains the label authority: decoding returns the emitted label, including the empty label, and encoding applies only the shared UTF8/NUL/size checks, so an unknown label fails server-side. No public API, dependency, version or TFM changes.
- Why it mattered: before E70, MySQL-compatible profiles could not read `information_schema.columns` or any enum-bearing view, because `routine_type`, `sql_access_data`, `security_type` and `column_key` are catalog enums and `sql_mode` is a catalog SET. `SELECT * FROM information_schema.columns` and the enum/SET columns of `information_schema.routines` now work; `information_schema.routines` still cannot be read as `*` because `EXTERNAL_NAME`/`SQL_PATH` are the `sys.binary` base type, whose `binaryin`/`binaryout` do not produce plain text (`'abc'::sys.binary::text` is `a`). That native binary contract is recorded, not guessed.
- Test-first correction: 21 offline cases (round trips including the empty label and comma-joined SET lists, NUL/unpaired-surrogate/wrong-CLR rejection, mislabelled category, base/element links, enum arrays, dynamic catalog rows, skipped inconsistent rows, unknown kinds, typed NULL, reader metadata and typed binding) and 8 LIVE cases. Two initial probes were rejected rather than encoded into tests: `information_schema.columns` never reports an empty `column_key` on this server (0 of 5,145 rows), so the empty-label case is exercised through a typed parameter and a `NULL::enum` cast instead of a view row; and a composite row cannot exist in the catalog, so that negative case moved to the loader.
- Independent review correction: review found a real availability defect in the first submission. A fixed name was classified by `typname` alone, so a user enum or SET named like a built-in (for example `public.text`) failed `ReadType`'s namespace check and retired every new session with "Invalid or inadequate PG built-in type catalog". Classification now requires the `pg_catalog` namespace too, with a test proving `pg_catalog.text` and a `public.text` enum coexist and stay distinct. Review also found that the dynamic branch had no other-session temporary-schema exclusion, so accumulated temporary enums could push the response past the exchange row limit; the query now excludes `pg_is_other_temp_schema`, and that one bounded bootstrap read passes its own row limit so the catalog bound is reachable and reports the catalog error rather than a generic exchange error. Namespace-qualified duplicate identity is now judged by namespace OID plus name because dots are legal in identifiers. Reader column schema now reports non-`pg_catalog` type names qualified, which fixes generated CommandBuilder/DataAdapter parameters that previously prefixed `pg_catalog.` to an enum name and failed late. The DbType constraint for enum/SET parameters now really follows the text family, as documented. Two review notes about test value were accepted: the mislabelled-layout cases were renamed to assert the loader skip they actually observe, and the live catalog-count assertion is scoped to the `information_schema` namespace so unrelated user enums cannot break it. Streaming `TextReader` input for enum parameters remains unsupported.
- Source-built LIVE: 489 cases pass on net10 (481 before E70) including the 8 new cases; the three-host run and final gate are recorded below. Verified per mode: catalog enum/SET row counts and metadata, MySQL-mode `information_schema.routines` enum/SET values, typed enum/SET parameters with server `22P02` label rejection, SequentialAccess enum labels and typed NULL enum/SET columns. Enum parameter binding requires the exact qualified `DataTypeName`; a compatible `DbType` still constrains it through the text-family mapping added in E69.
- Remaining scope: ordered per-type label caches, domain codecs beyond the four native `sys` shapes, enum arrays, composites, ranges and `sys.binary` are unimplemented. T23 remains In progress. No push, tag or publication.
